675: 49:## 1. Actual R3 word counts, even lift and a reusable edge argument
676: 50:
677: 51:Assume w avoids. Normalise c=1,A>=B by order-preserving automorphisms. Write w=R_1S_1...R_kS_k with
678: 52:S_i=sigma_(y_i)^(2g_i+1),rotation counts a_i,b_i,r_i=a_i+b_i,d_i=a_i-b_i,theta_i=y_(i-1)-y_i in Z_p,
679: 53:u_i=theta_i/2. Empty R_i imply theta_i!=0. Let H=sum g_i=p-1-kappa. Put
680: 54: R=p+3,F=3p-5,M=2p-R=p-3, k=F-2H=M+2kappa.                                              (1)
681: 55:M and k are EVEN. Z0 gives0<=B<=kappa. P/P0 at its actual N=4p-2,F>=1 domain gives
682: 56: F+sum floor(r_i/2)<=3p-2, hence sum floor(r_i/2)<=3.                                      (2)
683: 57:Let q=#nonempty runs,e=k-q,#nonempty EVEN runs=v. Then v<=3 and
684: 58: e=k-R+sum_(nonempty i)(r_i-1)>=2kappa-6+v.                                                (3)
685: 59:No purity/zero-free assumption is used.
686: 60:
687: 61:Let C=Z/(2p)Z and e0(t)=(p+1)t modulo2p be the UNIQUE EVEN lift of t in Z_p; it is well-defined/additive,
688: 62:since p+1 is even and p(p+1) is divisible by2p. For every proper pattern P define
689: 63: E(P)=B+sum_(i in P)d_i in[0,R], u_P=sum_(i in P)theta_i/2,
690: 64: alpha_i=e0(theta_i)-d_i, psi(P)=-B+sum_(i in P)alpha_i=e0(2u_P)-E(P).
691: 65:Always sum alpha_i=-D in C, D=A-B, because sum theta_i=0.                                   (4)
692: 66:
693: 67:When the W window is the FULL floor interval
694: 68: I(P)=[-floor((R-E)/2),floor(E/2)],
695: 69:its success is equivalent to psi(P) having a representative in[-R,0]. Forward: take t=2j, j in I(P)
696: 70:congruent to u_P; t is even in[-(R-E),E],represents e0(2u_P),and t-E represents psi in[-R,0].
697: 71:Conversely s in[-R,0] representing psi gives even t=s+E in[-(R-E),E], so t/2 is in I(P) and congruent to u_P.
698: 72:Thus the classes outside that interval have unique integer representatives in[1,M-1]. No E<=p restriction.
699: 73:
700: 74:EDGE-LIFT argument (proved here, applied separately below). Suppose on one or more CONNECT layers every
701: 75:psi(P) has a representative N(P) in an integer interval of width W0, with2W0+1<2p.
702: 76:An adding-i edge has difference N(P union{i})-N(P) representing alpha_i in[-W0,W0]. That interval has fewer
703: 77:than2p integers, so at most one represents alpha_i. Every index has an edge; orient it as adding i and
704: 78:call that integer h_i. All adding-i edges, on ALL those layers, share h_i; removing edges have-h_i.
705: 79:Connectivity gives a separate c_m with N(P)=c_m+sum_(i in P)h_i. Constants across layers need not agree.
706: 80:Since P^c belongs to the same layer,
707: 81: abs(sum_(i in P)h_i-sum_(i notin P)h_i)<=W0.                                              (5)
708: 82:Also |h_i|<=W0 and sum h_i=-D modulo2p. This argument needs ONLY the actually supplied N interval.
709: 83:
710: 84:On every such application h_i has parity d_i, hence r_i. An empty run has nonzero even h_i, magnitude>=2;
711: 85:an odd run has odd nonzero h_i. Zeros can occur only in nonempty even runs. Let z=#zeros,Z their set,
712: 86:E0 the empty set(|E0|=e),w_i=|h_i|,S=sum w_i. Then z<=v<=3,E0 disjoint Z, and
713: 87: w_i>=1+1_(i in E0)-1_(i in Z),
714: 88: S>=k+e+v-2z>=k+2kappa-6+2(v-z)>=k.                                                     (6)
715: 89:For kappa>=4, S>k. Equality S=k forces kappa3,e=v=z, and all pointwise weights minimal.
716: 90:Since at kappa3 also k=R, equality in sum_(nonempty)(r_i-1)>=v forces ALL odd nonempty runs singleton,
717: 91:ALL even nonempty runs of length2; odd labels have magnitude1,empty labels2,even nonempty labels0.          (7)
718: 92:This is a DERIVED equality case, not a label/run hypothesis.
719: 93:
720: 94:## 2. Interior lower family: p>=11, 3<=kappa<=M/2-1
721: 95:
722: 96:Set L=kappa+1,U=M/2. For EVERY m in[L,U] and EVERY integer E in[0,R],
723: 97: E-kappa+m-1>=E>=floor(E/2), p-m>=p-U=R/2>=floor(E/2).
724: 98:Hence gamma_m(E)=floor(E/2) at the ACTUAL R=p+3; all patterns on all these layers have the floor window.
725: 99:Avoidance/Section1 gives N(P) in[1,M-1], of width W0=M-2. CONNECT applies since1<=m and2m<=M<k.
726: 100:The edge-lift argument gives(5) with W0=M-2, because2M-3=2p-9<2p.
727: 101:
728: 102:We prove a NEW signed-sum contradiction. Start s_i=sign(h_i) on nonzeros, arbitrary at zeros, with sum s_i h_i=S.
729: 103:Let2m0 be the cyclic change count. If all nonzeros have one sign choose that same sign at zeros(m0=0).
730: 104:If they have both signs, every choice at zeros has m0>=1. The following cases are exhaustive.
731: 105:
732: 106:A. L<=m0<=U. The aligned signs already give S>=k>M-2, contradicting(5).
733: 107:
734: 108:B. m0>U. Its2m0 maximal constant-sign stretches alternate. Choose the odd/even family of m0 stretches
735: 109:with smaller total weight<=S/2, and flip f=m0-U lightest stretches in it. They are nonadjacent, f<m0,
736: 110:so exactly2f boundaries disappear. The new m is U and the signed sum is at least
737: 111: S U/m0>=S(2U)/k=SM/k>=M>M-2, since2m0<=k. No bound on the number of zero-weight stretches is needed.
738: 112:
739: 113:C. 1<=m0<L. Put f=L-m0. Let J be the union of stretch interiors, n=|J|>=k-4m0.
740: 114:Then n-2f>=M-2-2m0>=M-2-2kappa>=0, so LIGHT applies to an independent f-set in these disjoint paths.
741: 115:Interior adjacency in the original cycle lies in one such path(endpoints excluded), so flipping this set
742: 116:adds EXACTLY2f boundaries and gives m=L. Write gamma=2f/n<=1,e_J=|E0 intersect J|,z_J=|Z intersect J|.
743: 117:The new sum is at least
744: 118: W_(J^c)+(1-gamma)W_J
745: 119: >=k+e-z-2f-gamma e_J+gamma z_J
746: 120: >=k-2f-z+(1-gamma)e
747: 121: =M-2+2m0-z+(1-gamma)e.                                                                  (8)
748: 122:If m0>=2,2m0-z>=1. If m0=1,z<=1 the margin is positive; if z2, e>=2 and gamma<1 below.
749: 123:For m0=1,z3, n>=M+2kappa-4>3kappa, because M>=8 and kappa<=M/2-1<M-4.
750: 124:Thus gamma=2kappa/n<2/3, and (1-gamma)e>e/3>=1, strictly. This also supplies gamma<1 for z2.
751: 125:In every case the new sum>M-2. Boundary gamma1 at larger m0 is permitted: its coefficient is NONNEGATIVE,
752: 126:and the separate margin2m0-z is already strictly positive; no false strict-interior premise is used.
753: 127:
754: 128:D. m0=0. LIGHT on the full k-cycle with f=L gives2L changes(k>=2L) and sum>=S(1-2L/k)=S(M-2)/k.
755: 129:This is>M-2 if S>k. If S=k the sole remaining case is the one-sign kappa3 equality structure(7).
756: 130:Section5 below excludes that actual word. Thus the interior family is proved. []
757: 131:
758: 132:## 3. Lower-middle endpoint: p>=11, kappa=M/2=(p-3)/2
759: 133:
760: 134:Now k=2M=4kappa,kappa>=4,S>k by(6). Use ONLY m=kappa. Then p-m=R/2 and
761: 135: gamma_m(E)=min(E-1,floor(E/2)).
762: 136:For1<=E<=R-1 both sides are full floors. At E0 the W interval is[-R/2,-1], which forbids every even
763: 137:representative of psi in[-R,-2] but may permit psi0. At E=R its interval[1,R/2] forbids representatives
764: 138:in[-R+2,0] but may permit psi=-R, whose class has representative M. Thus ALL patterns on this layer
765: 139:have allowed N(P) in[0,M]. This is a relaxation, not an assertion that both endpoints are always allowed.
766: 140:
767: 141:Its width W0=M has2M+1=2p-5<2p; CONNECT applies2m=M<k. Section1 gives integer increments and(5) with
768: 142:bound M. We construct signs with exactly2kappa changes and signed sum>M.
769: 143:If m0=kappa, S>k>M. If m0>kappa, the light-stretch construction of Section2B gives new sum
770: 144:>=S*kappa/m0>=S(2kappa)/k=S/2>M.
771: 145:If1<=m0<kappa, put f=kappa-m0,J interiors,n>=k-4m0=4f. Thus gamma=2f/n<=1/2. LIGHT adds2f boundaries,
772: 146:and the same explicit weight calculation gives new sum
773: 147: >=M+2m0-z+(1-gamma)e >M.
774: 148:For m0>=2 the first margin>=1. For m0=1,z<=1 it is positive; for z2 the e term is positive; for z3,
775: 149:e>=2kappa-6+z>=5 and1-gamma>=1/2, so-1+(1-gamma)e>=3/2>0.
776: 150:If m0=0, LIGHT with f=kappa on the k=4kappa cycle gives sum>=S/2>M. All cases contradict(5).
777: 151:This proves the endpoint uniformly, including p11,kappa4. No interior-range RP2NE lemma is assumed. []
778: 152:
779: 153:## 4. Small prime p7,kappa3: an independent relaxed layer and its critical sign case
780: 154:
781: 155:Here R10,F16,H3,k10,M4. Use m3; CONNECT applies2m6<k10. Directly
782: 156: gamma_3(E)=min(4,E-1,floor(E/2)).
783: 157:For1<=E<=9 both E and10-E have full floors. At E0, I=[-4,-1]; at E10,I=[1,4]. The CRT forbidden
784: 158:representatives in either endpoint case are the even values in[-8,-2]; only classes0 and4 may be added
785: 159:to the ordinary complement[1,3]. Hence every pattern on F_3 has allowed N(P) in[0,4].
786: 160:Width4 has2*4+1=9<14; Section1 yields |h_i|<=4 and complement bound(5)<=4. Counts(6)-(7) still hold,
787: 161:with S>=10,e>=v>=z,z<=3. We now produce exactly6 changes and signed sum>4.
788: 162:

790: 164:>=S*3/m0>=S*6/10>=6>4. If m0=0, LIGHT with f3 gives sum>=S(1-6/10); this is>4 unless S10.
791: 165:That one-sign equality is excluded in Section5, not assumed absent.
792: 166:
793: 167:For m0=1/2, put f=3-m0,J interiors,n>=10-4m0. Thus n>=6 with f2 or n>=2 with f1; LIGHT applies.
794: 168:The exact lower calculation gives
795: 169: new sum>=4+2m0-z+(1-gamma)e, gamma=2f/n.
796: 170:For m0=2,2m0-z>=1 even at gamma1. For m0=1,z<=2 the margin is strict as before.
797: 171:For m0=1,z3, gamma<=2/3 and e>=3; strictness can fail in this estimate ONLY if n6,e3.
798: 172:If S>10, at least one pointwise weight exceeds the baseline1+1_E0-1_Z; both coefficients1 and
799: 173:1-gamma>=1/3 are positive, making the inequality strict. If S10,(7) gives e=v=z3 and minimal weights.
800: 174:If e_J<3 or z_J>0, the previously discarded gamma(e-e_J+z_J) is positive, again giving strictness.
801: 175:The sole critical case has n6,e_J3,z_J0,S10. Its six interior vertices contain THREE empty weights2
802: 176:and THREE singleton weights1. Three vertices in a union of paths contain a nonadjacent pair(a path has
803: 177:no triangle). Flip two such unit interior vertices. This adds4 boundaries, giving m3, and the new sum is
804: 178:10-2(1+1)=6>4. No scientific enumeration or LIGHT with n1 is used.
805: 179:All mixed-sign/nonminimal cases contradict(5); Section5 excludes the one-sign S10 exception. []

807: 181:## 5. One-sign minimum kappa3, valid in BOTH interior and p7 proofs
808: 182:
809: 183:Here k=R=p+3,S=k,all nonzero labels have one sign,and(7) holds:e=v=z<=3,odd runs singleton/even runs2,
810: 184:odd labels magnitude1/empty labels2/even nonempty labels0. Let B be after valid A>=B normalisation;
811: 185:Z0 gives0<=B<=3. Sum h_i=-D modulo2p,D=p+3-2B.
812: 186:Positive labels give sum h_i+D=2p+6-2B, divisible by2p ONLY when B3.
813: 187:Negative labels give sum h_i+D=-2B, divisible by2p ONLY when B0. Thus B1/2 are impossible.
814: 188:These are actual congruences, not an R2 classification or an input unit-label hypothesis.
815: 189:
816: 190:B3, all labels positive. At most2v<=6 rotation letters lie in even runs, so A=R-B=p supplies at least
817: 191:p-6>=1 positive singleton. For such index i,d_i=1,h_i=1 imply e0(theta_i)=2,theta_i=2,u_i=1.
818: 192:The SINGLETON pattern has m1,E=B+d_i=4 and E-=p-1. At kappa3,
819: 193: gamma_1(4)=min(p-1,1,2)=1,
820: 194: gamma_1(p-1)=min(p-1,p-4,(p-1)/2)=(p-1)/2 (p>=7).
821: 195:Its W interval[-(p-1)/2,1] contains1, contradicting avoidance. This window is evaluated separately;
822: 196:m1 is not silently included in the fixed layers of Sections2/4.
823: 197:
824: 198:B0, all labels negative. Every singleton has d_i=1,h_i=-1,theta_i=0,u_i=0. If e>=1, choose empty j,
825: 199:whose h_j=-2 implies theta_j=-2,u_j=-1. There are k-2v>=p-3>=4 positive singleton runs, and at most two
826: 200:are cyclic neighbours of j. Choose a NONADJACENT singleton i. P={i,j} has m2,E1,E-=p+2,u_P=-1.
827: 201:Directly gamma_2(1)=min(p-2,-1,0)=-1 and
828: 202: gamma_2(p+2)=min(p-2,p,(p+1)/2)=(p+1)/2.
829: 203:The LITERAL W interval[-(p+1)/2,-1] contains-1, even though its upper endpoint is negative. No false
830: 204:“window contains0” assumption is imposed here. This is a direct m2 test, not the full-floor CRT interval.
831: 205:If e0, then v=z0, all rotation runs are positive singletons and theta_i=0 for EVERY i. All reflection
832: 206:block values are equal; F=3p-5>=2p reflections of one sigma_y exist, and any2p of them multiply to1.
833: 207:Thus every one-sign exception is impossible. The theorem D3-L is complete. []

1047: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/proof-theoremP.md
1048: SHA256 58a85ebeba8208e2f89b39a691235d2d1374656d8888ea905fe96aa75e1dafc2
1049: LF 1-109
1050: 1:# Theorem P: a Chevalley–Warning proof of ordered EGZ for a class of dihedral words
1051: 2:
1052: 3:Status: written proof (lane MathOlson, 2026-10-16), mechanism checked by brute
1053: 4:force (`code/verify_marked.py`, `code/verify_theoremP.py`); NOT yet independently
1054: 5:reviewed.  This is a theorem about a CLASS OF WORDS in D_{2p}; it does not
1055: 6:prove ordered Olson for D_{2p} (the "many odd gaps" class remains open here).
1056: 7:
1057: 8:## Setting and conventions
1058: 9:
1059: 10:p is an odd prime. D_{2p} = {(v, e) : v in Z_p, e in {+1,-1}} with
1060: 11:(v, e)(v', e') = (v + e v', e e')  (rotations (x,+1), reflections (y,-1)).
1061: 12:For a word w = w_1 ... w_N and indices i_1 < ... < i_k the ordered product is
1062: 13:w_{i_1} w_{i_2} ... w_{i_k}.  An identity k-subword is such an index set with
1063: 14:product (0, +1).  Cyclic rotation of w preserves the existence of identity
1064: 15:k-subwords (a product U V becomes V U = U^{-1} (U V) U).
1065: 16:
1066: 17:Let w contain F >= 1 reflections.  Rotate w cyclically so that it ENDS with a
1067: 18:reflection:
1068: 19:    w = X_1 f_1 X_2 f_2 ... X_F f_F,
1069: 20:where f_t = (y_t, -1) are the reflections and X_t (t = 1..F) is the block of
1070: 21:rotations strictly between f_{t-1} and f_t (X_1 = rotations before f_1); n_t =
1071: 22:|X_t|.  ("Gap t" = X_t; cyclically these are exactly the gaps between
1072: 23:consecutive reflections.)
1073: 24:
1074: 25:## Theorem P
1075: 26:
1076: 27:If N = |w| <= 4p - 1 and  F + sum_{t=1}^{F} floor(n_t / 2) >= 3p - 1,  then w
1077: 28:has an identity 2p-subword.
1078: 29:
1079: 30:## Proof
1080: 31:
1081: 32:**Step 1 (parametrisation).** In each gap X_t list the rotations in index order
1082: 33:and group them into floor(n_t/2) consecutive PAIRS pi = (i, i'); if n_t is odd
1083: 34:the last rotation of X_t is a SINGLETON.  Introduce Boolean parameters
1084: 35:  u_2, ..., u_F  (gap colours; set u_1 := 0 and u_{F+1} := u_1 = 0),
1085: 36:  m_pi  for each pair pi  (pair marks).
1086: 37:Number of parameters:  M = (F - 1) + sum_t floor(n_t/2) >= 3p - 2.
1087: 38:
1088: 39:To a parameter point (u, m) assign the selection S(u, m):
1089: 40:* reflection f_t is selected iff u_t != u_{t+1}  (t = 1..F);
1090: 41:* both rotations of pair pi in gap t are selected iff m_pi XOR u_t = 1,
1091: 42:  otherwise neither;
1092: 43:* singletons are never selected.
1093: 44:
1094: 45:**Step 2 (injectivity; the empty selection).** The selection determines the
1095: 46:parameters: going left to right, u_1 = 0 and u_{t+1} = u_t XOR [f_t selected]
1096: 47:determine all u_t; then m_pi = [pi selected] XOR u_t.  Hence the map
1097: 48:(u, m) -> S(u, m) is injective, and S(u, m) is EMPTY iff (u, m) = 0: an empty
1098: 49:selection selects no reflection, so all u_t = u_1 = 0, and no pair, so all
1099: 50:m_pi = u_t = 0.
1100: 51:
1101: 52:**Step 3 (size).** |S(u,m)| = #{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} + 2 #{pi : m_pi XOR u_{t(pi)} = 1}.
1102: 53:The first term is the number of colour changes around the cycle 1..F, 1, hence
1103: 54:even.  So |S| is always EVEN, and |S| <= N <= 4p - 1.
1104: 55:
1105: 56:**Step 4 (signs and the product).** Let S = S(u,m).  For an element of S lying
1106: 57:in gap t or equal to f_t, the number of selected reflections strictly before
1107: 58:it is #{s < t : u_s != u_{s+1}}, which has the parity of [u_t != u_1] = u_t.
1108: 59:So in the ordered product every selected rotation x in gap t is multiplied by
1109: 60:c_t := (-1)^{u_t}, and a selected reflection f_t contributes c_t y_t to the
1110: 61:rotation coordinate.  The number of selected reflections is even (Step 3), so
1111: 62:the product is (V, +1) with
1112: 63:  V = sum_{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} c_t y_t + sum_{pairs pi selected} c_{t(pi)} x_pi,
1113: 64:where x_pi = x_i + x_i'.  Using (c_t - c_{t+1})/2 = u_{t+1} - u_t (which is
1114: 65:0 when u_t = u_{t+1} and c_t when they differ) and summation by parts with
1115: 66:u_1 = u_{F+1} = 0,
1116: 67:  sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_{t+1} - u_t) y_t = sum_{t=2}^{F} u_t (y_{t-1} - y_t).
1117: 68:For pairs: a selected pair (m XOR u_t = 1) contributes c_t x_pi; one checks
1118: 69:c_t x_pi [m XOR u_t] = x_pi m - x_pi u_t for both u_t = 0, 1.  Therefore, in Z_p,
1119: 70:  V(u, m) = sum_{t=2}^{F} beta_t u_t + sum_pi x_pi m_pi,
1120: 71:  beta_t = y_{t-1} - y_t - sum_{pi in gap t} x_pi.
1121: 72:V is a polynomial of degree 1 in the parameters (over F_p), and S(u,m) is an
1122: 73:identity subword iff V(u, m) = 0 in Z_p.
1123: 74:
1124: 75:**Step 5 (Chevalley–Warning on the Boolean box).** Write the size as the
1125: 76:integer polynomial
1126: 77:  Z(u, m) = sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_t + u_{t+1} - 2 u_t u_{t+1}) + 2 sum_pi (m_pi + u_{t(pi)} - 2 m_pi u_{t(pi)}),
1127: 78:which agrees with |S(u, m)| on {0,1}^M and has degree 2.  Over F_p put
1128: 79:  Q = (1 - V^{p-1}) (1 - Z^{p-1}),   deg Q <= 3(p - 1) < M.
1129: 80:On {0,1}^M, Q(x) = 1 if V(x) = 0 and Z(x) = 0 in F_p, and Q(x) = 0 otherwise
1130: 81:(Fermat).  Box lemma: for any polynomial Q over F_p of degree < M,
1131: 82:  sum_{x in {0,1}^M} (-1)^{|x|} Q(x) = 0.
1132: 83:(Proof: suffices for monomials; a monomial of degree < M omits some variable
1133: 84:x_j, and the involution flipping coordinate j pairs points with opposite signs
1134: 85:and equal values.)  Hence
1135: 86:  sum_{x : V(x) = 0, Z(x) = 0 mod p} (-1)^{|x|} = 0 in F_p.
1136: 87:The point x = 0 contributes +1, so there is a parameter point x != 0 with
1137: 88:V(x) = 0 and |S(x)| = Z(x) = 0 mod p.
1138: 89:
1139: 90:**Step 6 (conclusion).** By Step 2, S(x) is nonempty; by Step 3 its size is
1140: 91:even and at most 4p - 1; being divisible by p (odd), the size is a positive
1141: 92:even multiple of p below 4p, i.e. exactly 2p.  By Step 4 its ordered product is
1142: 93:the identity.  QED.
1143: 94:
1144: 95:## Remarks
1145: 96:
1146: 97:1. Corollary (reflection coset): any word of 3p-1 reflections in D_{2p} has an
1147: 98:   identity 2p-subword (F = 3p-1, all n_t = 0).  Finite-exact computations
1148: 99:   (m = 3..9) show 3m-1 is also the sharp threshold for composite m.
1149: 100:2. Which words are covered depends only on the R/F type pattern.  Uncovered:
1150: 101:   F - #(odd gaps) <= 2p - 2; this contains every extremal (length 4p-2)
1151: 102:   dihedral avoider found in the exhaustive D10 data (alternating odd blocks).
1152: 103:3. The 2-part (size parity) is handled ONLY through pairing inside gaps.  A
1153: 104:   general "mixed modulus" Chevalley–Warning statement that would also control
1154: 105:   parity is false (it would give D(G) = D*(G) for G = C_2^4 + C_6).
1155: 106:4. Prime powers m = p^k: the same argument with congruences mod p^k would need
1156: 107:   Brink's restricted-variable Chevalley theorem for prime-power moduli
1157: 108:   (Combinatorica 31 (2011) 127-130); its exact hypotheses have NOT been read
1158: 109:   from the source here, so the extension is CONDITIONAL and kept separate.
1159:

1195: 5:1. **Original order:** call the original word AB and the rotated word w′=BA. Cutting after a reflection only cyclically permutes the gap lengths, preserving F,N and sum floor(n_t/2). 15: 9:## 0. Exact scope, statements, logical order and accepted sources
16: 10:
17: 11:C3(p,kappa): p>=7 prime; a word w of length4p-2 over D_(2p), EXACTLY R=p+3 rotations in ONE nonzero
18: 12:pair{rho_c,rho_-c}; every maximal CYCLIC CONSTANT reflection block odd; kappa=p-1-sum floor(|S_t|/2) in[2,p-1].

51: 45:## 1. Actual even-R counts and capacity
52: 46:
53: 47:Assume C3 avoids. Normalise c=1,A>=B by the accepted order-preserving group automorphisms. Put
54: 48: C=h+1=R/2, Pi=h-2=(p-3)/2, H=p-1-kappa, F=3p-5=6h-8, k=F-2H=6h-8-2H.                    (D1)
55: 49:k and R are EVEN. Let N_1,...,N_q be nonempty rotation runs in cyclic order and M_i the reflection run
56: 50:between N_i,N_(i+1). Write |M_i| for its number of constant blocks. Adjacent values inside M_i differ,
57: 51:because intervening empty runs are genuine. Put x=R-q. P0 gives at least p-3 odd nonempty runs, so
58: 52: 0<=x<=6, q=R-x, d=max|d(N_i)|<=x+1.                                                     (D2)
59: 53:Any run has at most R-(q-1)=x+1 letters. Let u=ceil(x/2) in{0,1,2,3}.
60: 54:T=sum z_i d(N_i) is EVEN for every labelling. AFTER the BAL parity change(or already balanced),
61: 55:|T|<=d, hence |T|<=2u. This is NOT asserted of an arbitrary pre-change arc choice.
62: 56:
63: 57:A pair is a two-consecutive-block arc in one M_i; a singleton is one block. Pair capacity
64: 58: c=sum floor(|M_i|/2)=(k-o)/2, o=#{i:|M_i| odd}.
65: 59:Since k is even, o is even and o<=q (o<=q-1 when q odd). Therefore
66: 60: c>=p-4-H+ceil(x/2)=2h-5-H+u.                                                            (D3)
67: 61:Full pairing leaves ONE non-pair block in each odd M_i and none in an even M_i. A non-pair block may
68: 62:be unused or already a singleton; both allow a loss-free parity change. No run purity is assumed.
69: 63:
70: 64:## 2. Fresh R3 window, direct gain lemma and three-block optional gain
71: 65:
72: 66:For2m>=2 disjoint singleton/pair arcs put s=p-m. L3.1 gives {E+,E-}={C+j,C-j}, |T|=2j, and the ACTUAL
73: 67:W formula becomes gamma_m(E)=min(s,E+H-s,floor(E/2)).                                      (W3)
74: 68:For EVERY j<=u, both gamma are full floors on
75: 69: alpha_u=floor((C+u)/2) <= s <= beta_u=H+ceil((C-u)/2).                                   (F_u)
76: 70:Indeed this checks the largest floor(E/2) for s and the smallest ceil(E/2) for H+ceil(E/2)-s.
77: 71:Its exact width is H-u+eta_u, eta_u=(C-u) mod2. The floor sum
78: 72: Omega_j=floor((C+j)/2)+floor((C-j)/2)
79: 73:is h+1 if C+j is even, h otherwise. Thus ALWAYS Omega_j>=h; it is h+1 at signed T0 when h odd,
80: 74:or at signed T=+2 when h even. Call this OPTIMAL value tau=0(h odd),tau=2(h even).
81: 75:“tau labelling” below means SIGNED T=tau. Its actual arc system has |T|=tau, not an extra sign premise.
82: 76:
83: 77:Direct gain lemma GW3 (fresh proof). If both gamma>=0 and Gamma(s,T)+G>=p-1, where G=#pair arcs,
84: 78:then an identity2p-subword exists. Label arcs cyclically X_1,Y_1,...,X_m,Y_m. L2.1 gives proper patterns
85: 79:with exactly m intervals and u_P=(sum y_a-sum y_b)/2, E+ independent of endpoint choices. The u-values
86: 80:contain a CD sumset of G two-value sets and singleton sets, of size>=min(p,G+1); pair values differ by
87: 81:genuineness. The W interval contains0 and has Gamma+1 integers. If either set is all Z_p they meet;
88: 82:otherwise their counts sum>=p+1 and meet. W gives identity. No R2 GW theorem is imported. []
89: 83:
90: 84:Fresh optional3-block lemma. Reserve three consecutive blocks with nonzero successive differences
91: 85:delta1,delta2, disjoint from a base arc system. It may add0 or2 singleton arcs with extra u-values,
92: 86:up to fixed sign/halving,{0,delta1,delta2,delta1+delta2}. This is {0,delta1}+{0,delta2}, of at least3
93: 87:residues by CD. The insertions are even inside one empty-run region, so the labels of EVERY nonempty
94: 88:N_i and T are unchanged; the outer gap labels stay fixed. Endpoint telescoping in L2.1 proves the extra
95: 89:u-formula, for every base endpoint choice. Thus m rises by at most1 and guaranteed gain is2. []
96: 90:
97: 91:If all attained m lie in the full-floor window for a fixed tau and G+2+Omega_tau>=p-1, this family kills:
98: 92:its u-set has at least min(p,G+3) residues and its SAME floor interval has Omega_tau+1. This uses only
99: 93:W/L2.1/CD; no old ARC+/SEG or unreviewed segment generalisation is assumed. In each use base>=2 arcs.
100: 94:
101: 95:## 3. Both BAL constructions, with the actual free index and pair-loss direction
102: 96:
103: 97:Flexible construction: choose G0<=c pairs, then2m0-G0>=0 singletons on other blocks. This fits iff
104: 98:G0+2m0<=k. If k-2G0>0, choose the designated BAL index i at an M_i with a NON-PAIR/free block.
105: 99:There a parity change adds/deletes a singleton, never splits a pair. At its partner i'!=i use e5.
106: 100:The resulting G>=G0-1 and arc count2m0+Delta, Delta in{-2,0,2}; s' in{s0-1,s0,s0+1}.
107: 101:If a pair IS lost, its split adds an arc; the designated free-index move is+-1, so Delta>=0 and s'<=s0.
108: 102:If EVERY M_i has a non-pair block, both changes add/delete singletons and there is NO pair loss.
109: 103:Already balanced labels need no change. Every actual resulting system still has at least2 arcs below.
110: 104:
111: 105:Fixed-count construction: assume c>=G0+1, G0+2m0<=k-1 and2m0-G0>=1. In an M_l with>=2 blocks,
112: 106:designate its initial two blocks as one singleton and one UNUSED reserved block. Removing them lowers
113: 107:capacity by exactly1. Choose G0 pairs among the rest and the remaining2m0-G0-1 singletons away from
114: 108:pairs/reserved block. The budget ensures room. BAL is applied at l. If the partner adds an arc, DELETE
115: 109:the designated singleton; if it deletes, ADD the reserved singleton. I7 allows these applicable moves.
116: 110:Total arc count remains2m0; at most ONE pair is lost(at the partner). If all M_i are odd, every partner
117: 111:has a non-pair block, so one may choose the partner move without a split and NO pair is lost. []
118: 112:
119: 113:## 4. Fresh direct parity filling, with T0/T2 counting multiplicities explicit
120: 114:
121: 115:After full pairing of every remaining M_i (possibly reserving an initial3-block segment), each odd-length
122: 116:remainder has exactly one free block(bit a_i=0/1); each even remainder, including zero length, has none
123: 117:and is a RIGID edge. Let g_i=#fixed pairs, G=sum g_i. Choose any signs satisfying
124: 118: z_(i+1)=(-1)^(g_i)z_i on rigid edges,
125: 119:and set every free bit to realise that relation with g_i+a_i. This gives a valid base of G+xi=2m_b
126: 120:arcs, xi=sum a_i, since the product of edge relations is1. No extra parity condition is assumed.
127: 121:A complete fixed free-bit pattern determines z up to global sign. Thus it gives AT MOST TWO signedT0
128: 122:labellings, but AT MOST ONE signedT=+2 labelling. All bad-pattern bounds below use the correct factor.
129: 123:
130: 124:Fill lemma. Let r rigid edges and total rotation excess x=R-q satisfy r+x<=J, J in{2,4}, r<q and
131: 125:R-2J>J+1. Then both signed T0 and signed T=+2 can be attained. There are at least n>=R-2J choices;
132: 126:for J2,p>=11, at least C(R-4,3) choices for either target.
133: 127:Proof. Rigid edges form paths. Fix signs in each path up to component orientation, and include every
134: 128:non-singleton rotation vertex outside them as its own component. A component contribution has absolute
135: 129:value<=number of vertices plus total excess x, hence<=r+1+x<=J+1; an isolated deviant satisfies the same
136: 130:bound. Orient components greedily against the current sum to get |F_c|<=J+1, then flip all assigned signs
137: 131:if needed so0<=F_c<=J+1. All remaining vertices are FREE SINGLETONS, n>=q-(2r+x)>=R-2J.
138: 132:For tau0/2 the target tau-F_c has magnitude<=J+1<n and parity n(total rotation mass is even), so these
139: 133:free+-1 contributions attain it in at least n ways(binomial coefficients strictly inside their range).
140: 134:For J2, |tau-F_c|<=3 and n>=R-4>=10, so the number>=C(n,3)>=C(R-4,3). []
141: 135:The J4 uses below have p>=11, hence R-8>=6>5. J2 is also valid at p7,R10,n>=6>3.
142: 136:
143: 137:Two elementary counting bounds, proved here. For n>=10,
144: 138: C(n,3)>=12n>2n+8.                                                                       (B1)
145: 139:This follows from(n-1)(n-2)>=9*8. Binomial coefficients increase up to the middle, so interior near-
146: 140:central choices dominate C(n,3) or C(n,4) at the indices used below.
147: 141:For n>=12,
148: 142: 2C(n,4)>C(n+1,3)+n+2.                                                                   (B2)
149: 143:Indeed C(n,4)>=(n-3)C(n,3)/4>=2C(n,3); n+2<=C(n,2), and C(n,2)<=3C(n,3)/10, so the right side
150: 144:<=C(n,3)+2C(n,2)<=8C(n,3)/5<4C(n,3). All are symbolic inequalities, not scientific-data enumeration.
151: 145:
152: 146:One rigid edge with x0(all R coefficients+-1): write n=R-2=2h. The endpoint contributions are either
153: 147:constrained equal or opposite. The number of signedT0 labellings is at least2C(n,h-1); the number of
154: 148:signedT=+2 labellings is at least2C(n,h-2). For T0: equal endpoints give contributions+-2 and the remaining
155: 149:n signs sum to-2/+2, each C(n,h-1); opposite endpoints give2C(n,h)>=that bound. For T2: equal endpoints
156: 150:give C(n,h)+C(n,h-2), opposite endpoints2C(n,h-1), both>=2C(n,h-2). These count signed vectors, not just
157: 151:arc systems; the above factors TWO(T0)/ONE(T2) convert bad free-bit sets correctly.

434: 50:## 2. Arc systems (Lemma ARC) and optional segments (Lemmas ARC+, SEG)
435: 51:
436: 52:Arc: a nonempty set of consecutive blocks whose internal runs are all EMPTY; nu(X) := number of distinct values in X (>= 2 if
437: 53:X has an internal run).  Arc system: 2m >= 2 pairwise disjoint arcs X_1, Y_1, ..., X_m, Y_m in cyclic order; the gap after
438: 54:an arc (runs and blocks up to the next arc) contains the run right after the arc; gaps after X_i are IN, after Y_i OUT;
439: 55:E+ := B + sum of d_t over IN-gap runs.
440: 56:**Lemma 2.1.**  For a_i in X_i, b_i in Y_i, P := union of the runs R_(a_i + 1), ..., R_(b_i) is a proper pattern with exactly m
441: 57:intervals, E+(P) = E+, and u_P = (1/2) sum_i (y_(a_i) - y_(b_i)).
442: 58:Proof.  The i-th interval consists of internal (empty) runs of X_i after a_i, the IN-gap runs and internal runs of Y_i up to
443: 59:b_i; OUT-gaps contain a run, so intervals are separated and P != [k]; empty runs have d = 0; theta telescopes.  []
444: 60:A **segment** is a nonempty set Sigma of consecutive blocks inside one maximal reflection run (internal runs empty).  Given a
445: 61:base arc system and pairwise disjoint segments Sigma_j disjoint from all base arcs, with integers t_j >= 0, a configuration
446: 62:chooses blocks c_1 < ... < c_(2t'_j) in each Sigma_j (0 <= t'_j <= t_j) and adds them as single-block arcs.
447: 63:**Lemma ARC+.**  (i) Every configuration is an arc system with 2(m_b + sum t'_j) arcs and the base value of E+.  (ii) Its
448: 64:u-value is u_base + sum_j eps_j W_j, u_base in the base sumset {(1/2) sum_i (y_(a_i) - y_(b_i))}, eps_j = +-1 fixed by the

569: 13:whereas the proof needs the base value m_b in {m_lo, m_lo + 1} to satisfy m_b <= m_hi.  So the printed hypotheses do not
570: 14:support the printed proof when Pi_need < 0.  The raw statement is therefore CORRECTED (narrowed, explicitly) as follows.

688: 95:length 4p - 2 over D_(2p) whose rotation letters are all equal to one rho_x, x != 0 (B = 0 after normalisation), with exactly
689: 96:R = p rotation letters, kappa = (p + 1)/2, and every maximal cyclic constant reflection block of odd length.  Then w has an
690: 97:identity 2p-subword in increasing index order.  Proof: k = 2p - R + 2 kappa = 2p + 1 by (0.1) (a consequence, not a
691: 98:hypothesis), and 2p + 1 >= 3 kappa + 3 = (3p + 9)/2 iff p >= 7; Theorem K2P+.  The foundation reviewer's remark that B = 0 is
692: 99:not needed is recorded; the stated B = 0 scope of IIIb-X is not changed (the any-B statement is K2P+ itself).

694: 101:**I.11 (F11; Theorem ETA, statement precisified).**
695: 102:Statement.  Let w be in setting (S) with B = 0 and R <= 2 kappa + 1, so m* := kappa + 1 - floor(R/2) >= 1.  Choose the cyclic
696: 103:start so that the run R_1 is nonempty if R is odd (any start if R is even); index the blocks S_1, ..., S_k from there, put
697: 104:rho_j := r_1 + ... + r_j, eta_j := y_j/2 + rho_j in Z_p, and for a set Z of blocks eta(Z) := {eta_j : j in Z} (a set of
698: 105:residues).  If w avoids, then for every partition of {1, ..., k} into 2m* nonempty consecutive segments Z_1 < ... < Z_(2m*)
699: 106:(linear order), sum_i |eta(Z_i)| <= p + 2m* - 2.  (Such partitions exist: k - 2m* = 2p - 2 - [R odd] >= 0.)
700: 107:Proof details.  For a_i in Z_(2i-1), b_i in Z_(2i), P := union over i of the runs R_(a_i + 1), ..., R_(b_i): the intervals are
701: 108:nonempty, consecutive ones are separated by R_(b_i + 1), ..., R_(a_(i+1)), and the wrap-around gap contains R_1, so
702: 109:m(P) = m* and P is proper.  With B = 0, x := E+(P) = D_P = sum_i (rho_(b_i) - rho_(a_i)), and u_P - D_P =
703: 110:sum_i (eta_(a_i) - eta_(b_i)).  "G_(m*)" in v5 means gamma_(m*), evaluated directly (not by Fact 2.1):
704: 111:gamma_(m*)(E) = min(H + floor(R/2), E - floor(R/2), floor(E/2)), as E - kappa + m* - 1 = E - floor(R/2) and
705: 112:p - m* = H + floor(R/2).  The window I(P) = [-gamma_(m*)(R - x), gamma_(m*)(x)] is:
706: 113:  R even, 0 <= x <= R: {x - R/2};   R odd, 1 <= x <= R - 1: {x - ceil(R/2), x - floor(R/2)};
707: 114:  R odd, x = 0: {-floor(R/2)};      R odd, x = R: {floor(R/2)} (which misses x - floor(R/2)).
708: 115:If R is odd, R_1 is nonempty and not in P, so x <= R - 1.  Hence x - floor(R/2) lies in I(P) in every case, and avoidance
709: 116:(Lemma W) forbids sum_i (eta_(a_i) - eta_(b_i)) = -floor(R/2) (mod p).  These sums form the sumset
710: 117:eta(Z_1) - eta(Z_2) + ... - eta(Z_(2m*)), which by (CD) has >= min(p, sum_i |eta(Z_i)| - 2m* + 1) residues; it misses one, so
711: 118:sum_i |eta(Z_i)| - 2m* + 1 <= p - 1.  []
712: 119:
713: 120:**I.12 (Codex MINOR; Theorem SEG proof, and Corollary MID).**  In Theorem SEG replace "Lower the t_j minimally keeping total
714: 121:gain >= Pi_need: sum t_j <= Pi_need" by the following.  An optional pair is two single-block arcs and raises m by one; a base
715: 122:pair-arc is one arc.  The certified gain of a configuration family is G_b + sum_j g_j(t_j), G_b := number of base pair-arcs,
716: 123:g_j(t) := min(p - 1, 2t, b'_j - 1) (I.3).
717: 124:* Pi_need >= 1.  By Fact 2.2 (R >= p), Pi_need <= (p - 1)/2.  Start with all t_j = 0; while the certified gain is < Pi_need,
718: 125:  raise by one some t_j with 2t_j < b'_j - 1.  Such a j exists: if every t_j is saturated, either some g_j = p - 1 >= Pi_need, or
719: 126:  sum_j g_j equals the uncapped capacity and G_b + sum_j g_j = g_E - |L n O| >= Pi_need by (H3).  Each step raises the certified
720: 127:  gain by >= 1: before it, g_j(t_j) <= (certified gain) < Pi_need <= (p - 1)/2, so g_j(t_j) = 2t_j, and after it
721: 128:  g_j >= min(p - 1, 2t_j + 1) = 2t_j + 1.  Hence the loop stops after at most Pi_need - G_b <= Pi_need steps: sum_j t_j <= Pi_need.
722: 129:* Pi_need = 0 (R in {2p - 1, 2p}).  Take all t_j = 0.  (H1) gives m_hi >= m_lo + 1, so m_b in {m_lo, m_lo + 1} lies in
723: 130:  [m_lo, m_hi], and the window sum floor(E+/2) + floor(E-/2) >= ceil(R/2) - 1 = p - 1 already.
724: 131:* Pi_need < 0 (R >= 2p + 1).  Erratum 4 governs; Part II is the new branch.
725: 132:The lower window condition at m = m_b uses m_lo = max(1, kappa + 1 - ceil((R - d)/4)) >= kappa + 1 - ceil((R - d)/4) (both
726: 133:branches).  In Corollary MID (with erratum 1 e3) read every segment gain as clipped: the first pair of a segment with >= 3
727: 134:blocks gains min(p - 1, 2, b' - 1) = 2, a surviving base pair-arc gains 1, and afterwards each useful step adds >= 1 until
728: 135:Pi_need = (p - 1)/2 < p - 1 is reached; so sum_j t_j <= Pi_need - 1 as stated.

852: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/inverse-normalization-20261018/reflection/proof-K0star-r2.md

855: 12:## 0. Setting
856: 13:
857: 14:D_(2p) = {(v,e)}, (v,e)(v',e') = (v + e v', e e'), rho_x = (x,+1), sigma_y = (y,-1).  A word is cyclic; an identity 2p-subword is
858: 15:an increasing index set of size 2p with ordered product (0,+1); its existence is invariant under cyclic rotation
859: 16:(proof-theoremP.md, Setting) and under the automorphisms rho_v -> rho_(cv), sigma_v -> sigma_(cv+d), c != 0.  An avoider has no
860: 17:identity 2p-subword.
861: 18:
862: 19:**G-words.**  A G-word is a word of length N with F >= 1 reflections, R >= 1 rotations, all rotations in {rho_1, rho_-1}
863: 20:(normalised x = 1).  [r2 fix 3: R >= 1 for all maximal-decomposition statements below; a word with R = 0 is one cyclic
864: 21:reflection run (for k = 1 its sole "run" is empty with theta_1 = 0), it is handled by Lemma AR, and Lemmas GR0, H0 remain valid for
865: 22:every representation, R = 0 included.]  Cutting every maximal cyclic reflection run at every change of letter gives the maximal constant blocks
866: 23:S_1, ..., S_k (S_i = sigma_(y_i)^(l_i), l_i >= 1) and the rotation runs R_1, ..., R_k, R_i between S_(i-1) and S_i (indices mod k,
867: 24:y_0 := y_k); R_i has a_i letters rho_1 and b_i letters rho_-1, r_i = a_i + b_i >= 0, d_i = a_i - b_i, theta_i := y_(i-1) - y_i.
868: 25:R_i is an EMPTY run if r_i = 0; then y_(i-1) != y_i by maximality, i.e. theta_i != 0 (a *genuine* empty run).  g := number of
869: 26:empty runs.  w is RGR (in RGR_N(p) of proof-DR.md) iff g = 0.  More generally a *representation* is any cyclic alternation
870: 27:R_1 S_1 ... R_k S_k of constant blocks and possibly empty runs reproducing w (adjacent blocks may then have equal values).
871: 28:Parameters (of the maximal decomposition unless a representation is named): A = sum a_i, B = sum b_i, R = A + B, D = A - B,
872: 29:F = sum l_i, H = sum floor(l_i/2), kappa := p - 1 - H, o_F = #{i : l_i odd}, e_F = k - o_F, o_R = #{i : r_i odd},
873: 30:e_R = k - o_R (EMPTY RUNS COUNT AS EVEN ROTATION RUNS).  For N = 4p - 2:  F = 2H + o_F and R = 2p - o_F + 2 kappa.        (0.1)
874: 31:Patterns: P subset of [k] (a set of rotation runs, empty runs included); m(P) = number of maximal cyclic intervals of P; the
875: 32:boundary blocks of P are the S_j with exactly one of R_j, R_(j+1) in P (2m(P) of them); beta(P) = number of even boundary blocks;
876: 33:D_P = sum_(i in P) d_i; R_P = sum_(i in P) r_i; u_P := 2^(-1) sum_(i in P) theta_i in Z_p (additive; u_([k]) = 0; for a cyclic
877: 34:interval P = {i, ..., j}: u_P = (y_(i-1) - y_j)/2, so u_P != 0 iff its two boundary blocks have different values).
878: 35:Normalisation N: rho_v -> rho_(-v), sigma_v -> sigma_(-v) maps G-words to G-words, keeps every block length and every empty run
879: 36:(and its genuineness), swaps A and B; so WLOG A >= B.

881: 38:## 1. Exact criteria (re-derived for representations with empty runs)
882: 39:
883: 40:**Lemma GR0.**  For any representation (a_i, b_i, l_i, y_i) of any length, with f_i = [z_i != z_(i+1)], Phi = sum f_i,
884: 41:delta = #{i : l_i != f_i mod 2}, C_max = F - delta, E+ = sum_(z_i = +1) a_i + sum_(z_i = -1) b_i, E- = R - E+, s0 = 2p - C_max,
885: 42:s1 = 2p - Phi, Ach(z) = 2Z cap [max(-s1, s0 - 2E-, -E-), min(s1, 2E+ - s0, E+)] and tau(z) = -2^(-1) sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1)):
886: 43:w has an identity 2p-subword iff some z in {+1,-1}^k has Ach(z) cap (tau(z) + pZ) != empty.
887: 44:Proof (the proof of Lemma GR, proof-GR.md Sec. 2, re-checked line by line; it never uses r_i >= 1).  Cut w at the start of R_1.
888: 45:A selection with an even number of reflections has zone signs z_1 = +1, z_(i+1) = z_i (-1)^(c_i), c_i = number of letters chosen
889: 46:from S_i (so c_i = f_i mod 2, z defined also at empty runs, and z_(k+1) = z_1).  Multiplying from the left, a chosen rotation
890: 47:(x,+1) adds e x and a chosen reflection (y,-1) adds e y and flips e (e = current sign).  Inside S_i the chosen letters all equal
891: 48:sigma_(y_i), so they add z_i y_i if c_i is odd and 0 if c_i is even; the product is rho_V with V = sum_i z_i n_i +
892: 49:sum_(f_i = 1) z_i y_i (n_i = signed sum of chosen rotations of R_i, 0 for an empty run), and summation by parts gives
893: 50:sum_(f_i=1) z_i y_i = sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1))/2, so V = t - tau(z) with t = A+ - A- (numbers of chosen effectively positive /
894: 51:negative rotations).  The realisable totals C = sum c_i are exactly the even numbers in [Phi, C_max] (each c_i independently runs
895: 52:over f_i, f_i + 2, ..., l_i - [l_i != f_i mod 2] >= f_i), and for even t a pair (A+, A-) with A+ + A- = 2p - C, A+ <= E+,
896: 53:A- <= E- exists for some admissible C iff t is in Ach(z) (the computation of proof-GR.md Sec. 2, unchanged).  Every admissible
897: 54:data point is realised by a selection; global sign change z -> -z maps (Ach, tau) to (-Ach, -tau).  []
898: 55:**Lemma H0 (half windows).**  For a representation and a proper pattern P put
899: 56:  lambda(P) := min(p - m + 1, D_P + B - kappa + m - beta, floor((D_P + B)/2) + 1)   (m = m(P), beta = beta(P), kappa = p - 1 - H),
900: 57:  I(P) := [1 - lambda(P^c), lambda(P) - 1]  (integers; possibly empty),
901: 58:  I(empty) := [-min(p, A - kappa - 1, floor(A/2)), min(p, B - kappa - 1, floor(B/2))].
902: 59:Then w avoids iff u_P notin I(P) (mod p) for every proper P and 0 notin I(empty) (mod p).
903: 60:Proof.  Lemma GR0 with P = {z_i = +1}.  As sum theta_i = 0, tau(z_P) = 2^(-1) sum_i z_i theta_i = sum_(i in P) theta_i = 2 u_P,
904: 61:and 2 is invertible, so the condition for z_P is u_P notin [ceil(lo/2), floor(hi/2)] (mod p).  For P proper with Phi = 2m and
905: 62:beta even boundary blocks, delta = beta + (o_F - (2m - beta)), so c := C_max/2 = H + m - beta; E+ = A_P + B_(P^c) = D_P + B;
906: 63:hence floor(hi/2) = min(p - m, E+ + c - p, floor(E+/2)) = lambda(P) - 1, and E+(P^c) = E-(P), same m, beta, gives
907: 64:ceil(lo/2) = 1 - lambda(P^c).  P = empty (z = all -1): Phi = 0, c = H, E+ = B, E- = A gives I(empty); P = [k] gives -I(empty).
908: 65:No step uses the length or r_i >= 1.  []
909: 66:**Facts for G-avoiders of length 4p - 2** (F >= 1; if R = 0, Lemma AR of c2/invstar/proof-GR0-r1.md: 2p - 1 adjacent reflection
910: 67:pairs and EGZ; so R >= 1; a G-word with g >= 1 has k >= 2, F >= 2):
911: 68: (Z0) A, B <= 2p - 1 (2p equal rotations); after N, B <= kappa (if A, B >= kappa + 1 then 0 is in I(empty)); hence by (0.1)
912: 69:      o_F >= 2 kappa - B + 1 (R = A + B <= 2p - 1 + B).  (Pairs) kappa >= 0 (p disjoint pairs sigma_y sigma_y inside blocks, each
913: 70:      pair adjacent in the selection, give 1).  (V) kappa <= o_F - 1 (Davenport-alpha, proof-dihedral-partial.md Sec. 4, with
914: 71:      the H gaps "every second gap inside each block", pairwise non-adjacent; the gap at an empty run is not used; F >= 2,
915: 72:      N = 4p - 2 < 4p).
916: 73: (P0) e_R <= e_F + 2 kappa.  Proof: Theorem P (proof-theoremP.md; its gaps are the F gaps between consecutive reflections,
917: 74:      n_t = r_i at the gap of R_i and 0 elsewhere, empty runs included) gives F + sum_i floor(r_i/2) <= 3p - 2, i.e.

969: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/inverse-normalization-20261018/rotation/notes-GRX-v1.md
970: SHA256 afc6c31a2f9c9502655e3666595e535f4a003ccd7670ddbe993ebe4b620b1a30
971: LF 1-54
972: 1:# Lemma GRX: exact solvability criterion for EVERY word over D_(2p) (OB1)
973: 2:
974: 3:Owner OlsonRotationRigidity.  Written after host clock 2026-10-18T01:52:10-07:00.  Status [W] unreviewed; the
975: 4:statement is a direct generalisation of Lemma GR (olson/proof-GR.md Sec. 2, reviewed) and Lemma GR0
976: 5:(c2/invstar/proof-GR0-r1.md, unreviewed); the proof below is self-contained.  Controls [F] in Sec. 3.
977: 6:
978: 7:## 1. Representation and statement
979: 8:
980: 9:p odd prime, conventions of olson/proof-dihedral-partial.md Sec. 0.  Let w have F >= 1 reflections.  Read w
981: 10:cyclically and cut every maximal cyclic reflection run at each change of value: w is a cyclic alternation
982: 11:    R_1 S_1 R_2 S_2 ... R_k S_k,   S_i = sigma_(y_i)^(l_i) (l_i >= 1),   R_i = rotations with value multiset X_i,
983: 12:|X_i| = r_i >= 0 (R_i may be EMPTY; then y_(i-1) != y_i when the blocks are maximal, but the criterion below holds
984: 13:for every such representation, maximal or not).  y_0 := y_k, theta_i := y_(i-1) - y_i, sum theta_i = 0.
985: 14:For z in {+-1}^k: f_i = [z_i != z_(i+1)] (z_(k+1) = z_1), Phi = sum f_i (even), delta = #{i : l_i != f_i mod 2},
986: 15:C_max = F - delta, X^z := multiset union over i of z_i X_i (each value multiplied by z_i), R = sum r_i,
987: 16:T(z) := sum_{i : z_i = +1} theta_i (mod p), and Sigma_m(Y) := set of sums of size-m sub-multisets of Y.
988: 17:
989: 18:**Lemma GRX.**  w has an identity 2p-subword iff there are z and an even C with Phi <= C <= C_max, 0 <= 2p - C <= R
990: 19:and T(z) in Sigma_(2p-C)(X^z).
991: 20:(F = 0: identity 2p-subword iff 0 in Sigma_(2p)(X); rotations commute.  The sign pattern may be normalised z_1 = +1.)
992: 21:
993: 22:## 2. Proof
994: 23:
995: 24:A selection with an even number of reflections is given by (i) z_i := (-1)^(number of selected reflections
996: 25:cyclically before R_i, counted from a fixed cut point at the end of S_k), (ii) c_i := number of selected letters of
997: 26:S_i, with c_i = f_i (mod 2) [the parity of selected reflections in S_i is exactly what changes the zone sign between
998: 27:R_i and R_(i+1)], (iii) any sub-multiset of each X_i.  Conversely each such datum with 0 <= c_i <= l_i is realised
999: 28:(choose any c_i letters of the constant block S_i; any chosen rotations).  Because S_i is constant, its c_i selected
1000: 29:letters multiply to sigma_(y_i) (c_i odd) or 1 (c_i even); so the ordered product, read from the cut, is
1001: 30:    rho_V,  V = sum_i z_i (sum of chosen values in X_i) + sum_{i : f_i = 1} z_i y_i      (mod p),
1002: 31:by induction along the word (a rotation rho_x in a zone of sign z contributes z x; a reflection sigma_y entered in
1003: 32:a zone of sign z contributes z y and flips the zone sign; the even total makes the product a rotation).
1004: 33:Summation by parts: sum_{f_i=1} z_i y_i = sum_i y_i (z_i - z_(i+1))/2 = sum_i z_i (y_i - y_(i-1))/2 = -(sum_i z_i theta_i)/2,
1005: 34:and sum_i z_i theta_i = 2 sum_{z_i=+1} theta_i - sum_i theta_i = 2 T(z).  Hence V = sigma - T(z), sigma := the sum of
1006: 35:the chosen elements of X^z.  The size is C + m with C = sum c_i, m = number of chosen rotations.  For fixed z the
1007: 36:realisable C are exactly the even numbers in [Phi, C_max]: c_i ranges over the integers in [0, l_i] congruent to
1008: 37:f_i mod 2, i.e. from f_i up to l_i - [l_i != f_i mod 2]; summing, C ranges over all values of the right parity
1009: 38:between Phi and F - delta (each c_i moves in steps of 2), and Phi + C_max = F - delta + Phi is even because F = sum l_i
1010: 39:= delta + Phi (mod 2).  The rotations are chosen independently of the c_i.  So the selection is an identity
1011: 40:2p-subword iff C + m = 2p and sigma = T(z), as claimed.  The global sign change z -> -z maps X^z -> -X^z and
1012: 41:T(z) -> sum_{z_i=-1} theta_i = -T(z), so z_1 = +1 is a normalisation.  Identity subwords are invariant under cyclic
1013: 42:rotation of w (UV = 1 iff VU = 1), so the cyclic reading is exact.  []
1014: 43:
1015: 44:Specialisations: all rotation values in {1, -1} gives Lemma GR (Sigma_m(X^z) is then the window
1016: 45:{m - 2q : max(0, m - E+) <= q <= min(m, E-)}, E+- as in GR), and with empty runs Lemma GR0.
1017: 46:
1018: 47:## 3. Controls [F] (code/grx.py, code/rawdp.py, code/test_grx_v1.py; output tmp/test_grx_v1.out.json)

1046: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/proof-GR.md
1047: SHA256 33621e9e51e2f4f38ad68cff2a3a467c5277a2c6dc5317e591e35600f945fc08
1048: LF 11-61
1049: 11:## 1. Class
1050: 12:
1051: 13:RGR(p): words w of length 4p-1 over D_{2p} whose rotations all lie in
1052: 14:{rho_x, rho_-x} for one x != 0 and whose maximal (cyclic) reflection runs are each
1053: 15:constant (one letter sigma_{y_i} repeated; different runs may differ).  Normalise
1054: 16:x = 1 (automorphism rho_v -> rho_{v/x}, sigma_v -> sigma_{v/x}).  If F, R >= 1, w is a
1055: 17:cyclic alternation R_1 S_1 R_2 S_2 ... R_k S_k: R_i has a_i letters rho_1 and b_i
1056: 18:letters rho_{-1}; S_i = sigma_{y_i}^{l_i}, l_i >= 1; S_i lies between R_i and
1057: 19:R_{i+1} (indices mod k).  Theorem R is the subcase y_1 = ... = y_k.
1058: 20:
1059: 21:## 2. Lemma GR (exact criterion)
1060: 22:
1061: 23:For z in {+1,-1}^k put f_i = [z_i != z_{i+1}], Phi = sum f_i (even),
1062: 24:delta = #{i : l_i != f_i mod 2}, C_max = F - delta,
1063: 25:E+ = sum_{z_i=+1} a_i + sum_{z_i=-1} b_i,  E- = R - E+,
1064: 26:s0 = 2p - C_max,  s1 = 2p - Phi,
1065: 27:Ach(z) = 2Z intersect [ max(-s1, s0 - 2E-, -E-),  min(s1, 2E+ - s0, E+) ],
1066: 28:tau(z) = - 2^{-1} sum_i z_i (y_i - y_{i-1})  in Z_p.
1067: 29:
1068: 30:**Lemma GR.**  w in RGR(p) with F, R >= 1 has an identity 2p-subword iff some
1069: 31:z in {+-1}^k satisfies  Ach(z) intersect (tau(z) + pZ) != empty.
1070: 32:
1071: 33:**Corollary GR-W (window).**  If some z has |Ach(z)| >= p, then w has an identity
1072: 34:2p-subword.  (p consecutive even integers are pairwise distinct mod p.)
1073: 35:
1074: 36:### Proof of Lemma GR
1075: 37:
1076: 38:Selections with an even number of reflections <-> data (z; c_i; chosen rotations):
1077: 39:z_i is the sign of the zone of R_i (parity of selected reflections before R_i,
1078: 40:fixed up to one global sign), c_i is the number of letters chosen from S_i with
1079: 41:c_i = f_i mod 2, 0 <= c_i <= l_i.  As S_i is constant, sigma_{y_i}^{c_i} is sigma_{y_i}
1080: 42:(c_i odd) or 1, and the ordered product is rho_V with
1081: 43:V = sum_i z_i n_i + sum_{f_i = 1} z_i y_i,
1082: 44:n_i = (#rho_1 chosen in R_i) - (#rho_{-1} chosen in R_i).
1083: 45:Summation by parts (z_{i+1} = -z_i exactly when f_i = 1):
1084: 46:sum_{f_i=1} z_i y_i = sum_i y_i (z_i - z_{i+1})/2 = sum_i z_i (y_i - y_{i-1})/2.
1085: 47:Call a rotation of R_i effectively positive if it contributes +1 (rho_1 with
1086: 48:z_i = +1 or rho_{-1} with z_i = -1).  With A+, A- the numbers of chosen
1087: 49:effectively positive/negative rotations and t = A+ - A-, V = t - tau(z) (mod p).
1088: 50:Size: C + A+ + A- = 2p with C = sum c_i.
1089: 51:Realisable C: exactly the even numbers in [Phi, C_max] (start from c_i = f_i and
1090: 52:add pairs inside runs up to l_i - [l_i != f_i mod 2]; Phi <= C_max since each
1091: 53:run satisfies f_i <= l_i - [l_i != f_i]).
1092: 54:For even t, (A+, A-) = ((2p-C+t)/2, (2p-C-t)/2) is admissible iff
1093: 55:C <= 2p - |t|, C >= 2p + t - 2E+, C >= 2p - t - 2E-; together with Phi <= C <= C_max
1094: 56:(all bounds even) a C exists iff |t| <= s1, s0 - 2E- <= t <= 2E+ - s0,
1095: 57:t <= E+ and t >= -E- (the last two from 2p+t-2E+ <= 2p-|t| and its mirror).
1096: 58:So the realisable t for pattern z are exactly Ach(z), and V = 0 iff t = tau(z)
1097: 59:mod p.  Global sign change z -> -z maps Ach to -Ach and tau to -tau, consistent
1098: 60:with the realised first zone being +.  Identity subwords are invariant under
1099: 61:cyclic rotation, so the cyclic run model is exact.  []

86: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/reflection-round2/proof-Rp-integer-lift-v1.md
87: SHA256 34cb5e007095b6d3c9c9eb91b2385c9e52cd94f78cd5b82c73bac8a31961df69
88: LF 80-114
89: 80:## 3. Connectivity of the pattern graph (proved here)
90: 81:
91: 82:For 1 <= m with 2m < k, let F_m be the proper nonempty subsets of the k-cycle with exactly m cyclic intervals; join two
92: 83:patterns by an edge when they differ at exactly one run index and both belong to F_m.
93: 84:
94: 85:**Lemma CONNECT.**  The graph F_m is connected.  Every run index i is toggled by at least one edge of F_m.
95: 86:Proof.  A pattern has exactly 2m boundaries between adjacent run indices, lying at distinct block sites of the k-cycle;
96: 87:these boundaries alternate outside-to-inside and inside-to-outside.  Moving a boundary one site into a free adjacent site
97: 88:without colliding with another boundary toggles exactly one run and leaves the interval count m unchanged.
98: 89:We prove that any two such boundary configurations with the alternating labels are linked by these moves, including a
99: 90:pattern and its complement.
100: 91:
101: 92:Put n=2m.  Mark one outside-to-inside boundary as number 1; in cyclic order mark the other boundaries 2,...,n.  Choose
102: 93:integer lifts of their positions satisfying
103: 94:   x_1 < x_2 < ... < x_n < x_1 + k.
104: 95:Labels alternate with the number.  Every pattern can be represented this way, with boundary 1 chosen at any of its
105: 96:outside-to-inside boundaries.  The feasible lifted configurations form
106: 97:   L = {x in Z^n : x_(i+1)-x_i >= 1 for 1<=i<n, x_1+k-x_n >= 1}.
107: 98:A feasible unit change of one coordinate projects to the allowed boundary move above.  We show L is connected.
108: 99:
109: 100:Given x,y in L, z_i := min(x_i,y_i) also lies in L.  For consecutive coordinates this follows from
110: 101:min(x_(i+1),y_(i+1)) >= min(x_i+1,y_i+1) = z_i+1.  For the wrapping inequality, if z_1=x_1, then z_n<=x_n gives
111: 102:z_1+k-z_n>=x_1+k-x_n>=1; the other case is the same.
112: 103:If x != z, put S={i:x_i>z_i}.  A coordinate i in S can be decreased by one whenever its preceding cyclic gap is >1;
113: 104:this preserves the preceding gap, and enlarges the following one.  If that preceding gap equals 1, its predecessor is
114: 105:also in S: writing x_0=x_n-k and z_0=z_n-k, x_i=x_(i-1)+1 and z_i>=z_(i-1)+1 together with x_i>z_i imply
115: 106:x_(i-1)>z_(i-1).  If no coordinate of S were decreasable, following predecessors would force every coordinate into S and
116: 107:every gap to equal 1.  Their sum would give k=n, contrary to 2m<k.  A decreasable coordinate therefore exists; repeated
117: 108:steps reduce sum_i(x_i-z_i) until x=z.  Likewise y can be decreased to z, giving a unit-move path x to y by reversing
118: 109:that second path.  Thus L, and hence F_m, is connected.  For the complement of a pattern choose boundary 1 at one of its
119: 110:old inside-to-outside boundaries; the same argument applies (no claim that the old labels remain at their old sites).
120: 111:
121: 112:There is at least one allowed boundary move because n<k leaves a free site adjacent to some boundary.  It toggles some
122: 113:index t.  Rotate the whole configuration by i-t to obtain an edge toggling any prescribed i.  []

125: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/reflection-round2/proof-Rp-integer-lift-v1.md
126: SHA256 34cb5e007095b6d3c9c9eb91b2385c9e52cd94f78cd5b82c73bac8a31961df69
127: LF 140-148
128: 140:## 5. A cyclic signed-sum lemma
129: 141:
130: 142:**Lemma LIGHT.**  Let n nonnegative weights be arranged on a cycle, with n>=2f, f>=1.  There is an independent set of f
131: 143:vertices (no two cyclically adjacent) of weight at most f/n times the total weight.  The same conclusion holds for a
132: 144:disjoint union of paths with n vertices (use the vertices in their path order).
133: 145:Proof.  The set {0,2,...,2(f-1)} is independent on the n-cycle: its wrapping gap is n-2(f-1)>=2.  Its n cyclic translates
134: 146:all have f vertices and each vertex belongs to exactly f translates.  Their average weight is f/n times the total, so
135: 147:one is no heavier than that.  For a disjoint union of paths, list the paths consecutively and add edges to turn this list
136: 148:into one cycle; independence in the augmented cycle implies independence in the original paths.  []

154: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/inverse-normalization-20261018/rotation/proof-ROT-few-E-v4.md
155: SHA256 ac560eb81b0e17af5cad69345d7498fd431bc8f3c7e94b56701509983cb9ea8f
156: LF 28-43
157: 28:## 0. Representation
158: 29:
159: 30:Split every maximal cyclic reflection run of an avoider w into its maximal constant sub-blocks; consecutive sub-blocks
160: 31:of one run are separated by EMPTY rotation runs and have different values.  o := the number of odd sub-blocks.
161: 32:Normal form: F >= 1, since F = 0 would leave R = 4p-2 >= 3p-1 rotations and EGZ (twice) would give an identity
162: 33:2p-subword.  Apply PM* across every even sub-block (it allows empty runs); whenever two blocks of the same value become
163: 34:separated by an empty run, read them as one block (its length is the sum of theirs, so it is odd exactly when exactly
164: 35:one of them is odd) and apply PM* again if it is even; each such step lowers the number of odd blocks by 2 or leaves it
165: 36:unchanged (e.g. A | E1 | E2 with A odd, E1, E2 even and E2 of A's value: PM* across E1 lets A and E2 fuse into one
166: 37:odd block), so k <= o and k = o (mod 2).  The process ends with an avoider w^ of the same length and rotation multiset, given EITHER by a cyclic
167: 38:representation with 1 <= k <= o blocks, all odd, in which every EMPTY run separates two blocks of DIFFERENT values,
168: 39:OR (no odd block left, possibly after an equal-valued merge) by ONE EVEN block.  The even-block case is impossible:
169: 40:accepted Proposition K1 forces F = 2p-1, which is odd (equivalently, Davenport-alpha with H = F/2 and F >= 2 gives
170: 41:R + F/2 <= 3p-2, i.e. F/2 >= p, and 2p reflections of that block form an identity 2p-subword).  If k = 1, Proposition
171: 42:K1 makes all rotations equal.  So let k >= 2.  As before H = (F - k)/2, kappa = p - 1 - H, R = 2p + 2kappa - k, and Lemma DAV
172: 43:(boundary gaps are never in I; the frame is a boundary gap) gives kappa <= k - 1; GRX gives (ZP).

Residual-class consequences still require the actual residual hypotheses.
341: 14:

If every integer cell 1<=g<=g0, 0<=b<=B0, g+b>=2 satisfies one of the six clauses below, every maximal cyclic reflection run of w0 is constant. An empty box gives the conclusion by contradiction with the RED terminal.
345: 18:   Set kappa=g+b, R=D0+2b, k=2p-D0+2g, Delta=p+kappa-1-R. Clauses:

577: ## /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/institute/runs/six-lane-2026-10-16/review/olson-cj-cjx-parent-verdict-v1.md
578: SHA256 5cfd6a4fe15cab133e210cd6f6966e34fa633d1a8c432b79eb537c0016145f6d
579: LF 1-41
580: 1:# Olson reflection: B0S-CJ and its strengthened range — parent verdict v1
581: 2:
582: 3:Recorded at 2026-10-18T10:51:53-07:00.
583: 4:
584: 5:**CORRECTNESS-ACCEPTED, written partial theorems, with the mandatory corrections below.** Not full REFL(p), INV*(p), ordered Olson, a breakthrough gate, or a novelty claim. No Lean verification.
585: 6:
586: 7:## Exact setting and accepted scope
587: 8:Let p≥5 be prime, and let w be a word of length4p−2 over D_2p. Its R≥1 rotations are all equal to one nonidentity rotation, normalized to ρ1 (B=0); F≥1. Every maximal constant reflection block has odd length, and empty intervening rotation runs must be genuine (the adjacent reflection values differ). Let k be the number of these blocks, H=(F−k)/2, κ=p−1−H≥1, and Δ=k−p−κ−1=p+κ−1−R.
588: 9:
589: 10:For a proper cyclic interval S=[a,b] of runs let O(S)=[b+1,a−1], and m be rotation mass. S is heavy if m(S)≥2Δ−1. It is admissible if m(O(S))≥2Δ−1 and either O(S) has one run, or it has a run x other than its last run for which both m([b+1,x+1]) and m([x,a−1]) are at least2Δ−1. Prefixes/suffixes here are in the linear order of O(S), not a second traversal of the cycle.
590: 11:
591: 12:- **B0S-CJ:** if R≥4κ+1 and either Δ≤0 or there are two disjoint heavy admissible intervals, w has an increasing-index identity2p-subword.
592: 13:- **Strengthened B0S-CJ triple-prime:** the same conclusion holds under R≥4κ−4 **and** 2κ≤p−1. Both conditions are retained.
593: 14:- **Corollary1, corrected:** a partition of the cycle into three **nonempty** consecutive arcs, each of mass≥2Δ−1, supplies two disjoint heavy admissible intervals.
594: 15:- **Corollary2:** under the respective R hypothesis, there is no avoider if Δ≤1 or p≥κ+5Δ−5. The sufficient shortcut is 4Δ≤2κ+7 under R≥4κ+1, but only 4Δ≤2κ+2 under the strengthened hypothesis. The p≤23 conclusion of the original corollary does **not** transfer wholesale to the strengthened version.
595: 16:
596: 17:These statements apply to the specified B=0 setting only. RES_<(p) consequences import its accepted normalization into that setting. They do not normalize arbitrary rotations.
597: 18:

606: 27:## Mandatory corrections and disposition
607: 28:Read the immutable texts through this overlay; no certificate or scientific output is changed.
608: 29:
609: 30:1. **Original Corollary2, p≤23.** Replace the parameter-enumeration justification by this written argument. A violating triple satisfies 3κ+2+Δ≤p≤κ+5Δ−6, so4Δ≥2κ+8. With Δ≤κ−1, this forces κ≥6. Ifκ=6 then Δ=5 and p=25, not prime. Ifκ≥7 then Δ≥6 and p≥29. Thus no such triple has prime p≤23. RES_<(p)'s accepted B=0 setting is an explicit import; k≤p falls into the Δ≤1 branch.
610: 31:2. **Corollary1.** Insert “nonempty” before “consecutive arcs.” For Δ≥1 positivity already forces nonemptiness; the explicit word is needed in the degenerate Δ≤0 statement.
611: 32:3. **Strengthened proof, greedy step.** Apply 2|P′|+2≤Φ(P′)≤2κ to P′, yielding |P′|≤κ−1 and hence |P|≤κ after adding one run. Properness also follows from P⊆Z⊊[k]. The omitted possibility of the added run bordering the same interval on both sides would make P=[k], so P⊆Z excludes it. Thus the +1/0/−1 interval-count trichotomy used by the increment lemma is complete.
612: 33:4. **Strengthened Δ≤0 branch.** Use item5's SIGMA-2P argument followed by item6's rechecked constructive contradiction, not an invocation of the original B0S++ theorem with its stronger R hypothesis. Item7's import list includes those two steps as well as C1/C2/F1/F2.
613: 34:5. **Remaining notation/status notes.** In CJ's x* argument, “[x*+2,a−1]” means the part of O after x*+1 (empty at the endpoint). For the (29,7,6) remark, the |O|=1 case is impossible since its mass≤1<11. The k=1 example precedes the trivial R≥2p cut and illustrates the common Δ≤0 branch only. Strengthened r4 references to CJ-r1 are read through CJ-r2's errata. Historical “r3/UNREVIEWED” headers do not override the r4 pin and this verdict. The parity observation δ_P≤R is sufficient for L3; no stronger assertion is required.
614: 35:

H0's conversion of a selected interval pattern to an ordered identity subword, P0, and the earlier constructive cases retain the scoped imports recorded in the reviewed texts. Both reviewers independently checked the written mathematics; agreement is not a formal-kernel proof.
